### Assistant

_source line 309_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Progress update:

**LoRA adapters:**
- Qwen exp021 LoRA: 129MB / ~355MB = 37%. At ~390KB/s, about 10 min remaining.
- LLaMA SciERC LoRA: 126MB / ~340MB = 37%. At ~370KB/s, about 10 min remaining.

**Base models (ModelScope):**
- Qwen3-8B: shard 2 at 7% (259MB/3.72GB). Multiple shards downloading at ~800KB/s each. 

The Qwen model has 5 shards totaling about 18.5GB, and since they're downloading in parallel, the bottleneck is the slowest single shard—at 800KB/s, each 3.72GB shard takes roughly 78 minutes, so the full download should finish in about 1-1.5 hours. The LLaMA model is similarly progressing through its shards at comparable speeds.

Since the base model download is the limiting factor, I'm wondering if I can leverage vLLM's LoRA support to run inference directly without merging the adapter first. vLLM 0.18.1 has an `enable_lora` parameter that might let me load the base model and LoRA separately, which would let me start testing while the download completes. But modifying the existing code carries risk, so the safer bet is probably just waiting for the base model to finish downloading.

Actually, I'm realizing the ModelScope download is surprisingly efficient—hitting 800KB/s to 1MB/s per shard with parallel downloads giving me 3-5MB/s aggregate throughput. That's 2-3x faster than trying to transfer the merged model across servers via rsync, so I should just stick with the direct download approach.

While that's running, I'll get ahead by preparing the merge and inference scripts, and double-checking that the exp021 adapter is configured to point to the correct base model location.
```

     import sys, torch
     from peft import AutoPeftModelForCausalLM
     from transformers import AutoTokenizer
     adapter_path = sys.argv[1]
     output_path = sys.argv[2]
     model = AutoPeftModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(adapter_path, torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16, device_map="cpu", low_cpu_mem_usage=True)
     merged = model.merge_and_unload()
     merged.save_pretrained(output_path, max_shard_size="5GB")
     tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(adapter_path)
     tokenizer.save_pretrained(output_path)
     ```

The rsync is doing the whole directory, which includes 3 checkpoint dirs × ~977MB each ≈ 2.9GB of unnecessary data. That would take ages at this speed.

Since the adapter_model.safetensors is almost done, I should:


- Let it finish, then kill the rsync before it wastes time on those checkpoint directories
- Separately grab just the tokenizer files I actually need for the merge

For the merge to work, I need adapter_config.json (already there), adapter_model.safetensors (almost done), and the tokenizer files—so I can skip waiting for everything else to transfer. lora.py script loads the tokenizer from the adapter path. The tokenizer files in the LLaMA LoRA dir are: tokenizer.json (17M), tokenizer_config.json (50K), special_tokens_map.json, chat_template.jinja. These are needed.

Wait, but merge_lora.py uses `AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(adapter_path)` - if the tokenizer files aren't there, it can fall back to the base model's tokenizer. Actually it would fail.

Let me think about this more carefully. The rsync is doing whole-file transfer mode (-W), so it downloads one file at a time. After adapter_model.safetensors finishes, it'll go to the next file (alphabetically or by rsync ordering). The checkpoint dirs contain optimizer states etc. which we don't need.

I should wait for both adapters to finish their safetensors downloads, then kill the LLaMA rsync and manually copy over just the tokenizer files since they're small. The Qwen rsync was explicit about which files to grab, so it won't pull anything unnecessary.

Both are around 85-88% done on the safetensors at roughly 500KB/s, so Qwen should finish in about 2 minutes and LLaMA in about 1.5 minutes. I'll check back in a couple minutes. So it looks like ModelScope is downloading all the shards simultaneously, which explains why they're all progressing at similar rates. The Qwen base model's largest shard will take around 40 more minutes to finish, and LLaMA's largest shard will need about 68 minutes, so I should focus on getting the LoRA transfers done first and then check if there's a pre-merged model available to save time. I'm checking whether there's already a merged model sitting in the merged directories on autodl-5090 that I could use directly.

Actually, earlier shard 4 was 2.97G and shard 5 was 1.16G. 

Shard 5 should be nearly done by now—it was at 44% about 10 minutes ago, and at the current download speed it only needs another 12 minutes or so to finish. Meanwhile, the LLaMA downloads are progressing at a slower rate, with shard 4 at 64% and shard 1 still early at 15%.

I'm realizing the new download process that started 6 minutes ago should be resuming from where the old one left off, but the current shard sizes (725M for shard 1, 711M for shard 4) are actually higher than the ~650M the previous process had downloaded, which suggests the new process is making progress beyond what was already there.

Looking at the download speeds, LLaMA is only getting around 470kB/s per shard while Qwen is pulling 890kB/s, which means the total bandwidth is being split across concurrent downloads. At this rate, shard 1 alone will take over 2 hours to complete, even though shard 4 should finish in about 15 minutes. I'm wondering if the speed will improve once the faster shards finish and free up bandwidth, but this is clearly taking much longer than expected. I'm considering whether it would be faster to merge the model on the current machine and then transfer just the merged result instead of waiting for all the shards to download.

Actually, I just realized the merged LLaMA SciERC model already exists on autodl-5090 from a previous run. Rather than waiting for these downloads to crawl along, I should check if I can rsync the already-merged models from that server instead. Even at 500KB/s, transferring a 16GB merged model would take around 9 hours, but that might still be faster than waiting for all these individual shards to download at their current glacial pace. Let me see what merged models are actually available on that machine. the transfer bandwidth between the two AutoDL servers is ~500KB/s-1MB/s. But the ModelScope download speed is also ~500KB/s-1MB/s per file (but with parallel downloads). So the total effective bandwidth to ModelScope might be higher (4-5 parallel downloads at 500KB/s each = 2-2.5MB/s total).

For Qwen with 5 shards in parallel:
Total size: ~16GB
At 5 × 850KB/s ≈ 4.25MB/s total
Time: 16000/4.25 = 3765 seconds ≈ 63 minutes total from start
It's been 16 minutes, so ~47 min remaining.

For LLaMA with 4 shards in parallel (safetensors only):
Total size: ~15GB
At 4 × 470KB/s ≈ 1.88MB/s total
Time: 15000/1.88 = 7979 seconds ≈ 133 minutes from start
It's been about 6 min since restart, so ~127 min remaining (2+ hours).

The ModelScope approach is still faster than rsync of merged models which would take 9+ hours. I'll monitor the progress—Qwen should finish in roughly 45-50 minutes and LLaMA in about 2 hours. I'm wondering if the LLaMA download speed dropped because the restart required renegotiating the connection, so I should check whether the partial files from before are being reused or if they're being re-downloaded from scratch.

Starting FewNERD inference...\"\n                    nohup bash run_fewnerd_seed456.sh > /tmp/inference_fewnerd.log 2>&1 &\n                    echo \"$(date): FewNERD inference PID=$!\"\n                fi\n                QWEN_DONE=1\n            else\n                echo \"$(date): Qwen download process ended but model files not found!\"\n                QWEN_DONE=1\n            fi\n        fi\n    fi\n\n    # Check if LLaMA download process is still running\n    if [ \"$LLAMA_DONE\" -eq 0 ]; then\n        if ! pgrep -f \"snapshot_download.*Meta-Llama\" > /dev/null 2>&1; then\n            if ls /root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/LLM-Research/Meta-Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct/model-00001-of-00004.safetensors > /dev/null 2>&1; then\n                echo \"$(date): LLaMA download complete! greedy_macro_f1\\nzero_f1_mask = (all_sample_f1s.max(axis=1) == 0.0)\\nzero_f1_rate = float(zero_f1_mask.mean())\\n\\nprint(f\\\"Greedy macro F1: {greedy_macro_f1:.4f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"Oracle macro F1: {oracle_macro_f1:.4f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"Headroom: {headroom:.4f}\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"Zero-F1 rate: {zero_f1_rate:.4f} ({int(zero_f1_mask.sum())}/{n_total})\\\")\\n\\n# ===== Phase 2: 5 signals (instance-level) =====\\nprint(\\\"\\\\nPhase 2: Computing 5 signals...\\\")\\nlp_values = np.zeros(n_total)\\nsj_values = np.zeros(n_total)\\nfk_values = np.zeros(n_total)\\nem_values = np.zeros(n_total)\\nvc_values = np.zeros(n_total)\\n\\nfor i, inst in enumerate(instances):\\n    samples = inst[\\\"samples\\\"]\\n    n = len(samples)\\n    \\n    # LP\\n    lps = [s.get(\\\"mean_logprob\\\") for s in samples if s.get(\\\"mean_logprob\\\") is not None]\\n    lp_values[i] = float(np.mean(lps)) if lps else float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    \\n    # SJ\\n    sj_values[i] = structural_consistency_soft_jaccard(samples, subtask=\\\"ner\\\")\\n    \\n    # FK\\n    fk_values[i] = fleiss_kappa_surface(samples, subtask=\\\"ner\\\")\\n    \\n    # EM (pairwise exact match rate)\\n    sample_keys = []\\n    for s in samples:\\n        keys = frozenset((e.get(\\\"text\\\",\\\"\\\"), e.get(\\\"type\\\",\\\"\\\")) for e in s.get(\\\"entities\\\", []))\\n        sample_keys.append(keys)\\n    match_count = sum(1 for a in range(n) for b in range(a+1, n) if sample_keys[a] == sample_keys[b])\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.71}}"}

Good, I see the DGS analysis script structure. It takes `--datasets` as name:path pairs and supports bootstrap CI. However, this uses `gold` entities directly for degeneracy detection, which is oracle-based (not realistic). Let me check how the actual DGS selection works.

Wait, `dgs_select` computes sample_f1s using gold entities, then checks if all F1s are identical (degenerate). If degenerate, it falls back to greedy; otherwise it selects based on LP (mean_logprob). This IS oracle-based since it checks degeneracy using gold labels.


The degeneracy check itself just compares whether all sample F1 scores round to the same value — if they do, the instance is considered degenerate and DGS skips LP-based selection. But this fundamentally requires gold F1 scores to work, so the real DGS implementation in the paper must use a different non-oracle signal for detecting degeneracy.

I'll hold off on diving deeper into the analysis for now and wait for the inference outputs to finish downloading, then build the analysis scripts once I have the data ready. I'm checking back in about 15 minutes, aiming for around 3:55-4:00.

{
  "server": "autodl-5090-c",
  "command": "cat > /root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/code/analyze_generic_5signal.py << 'PYEOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Generic 5-signal SCS analysis with DGS.\nUsage: python analyze_generic_5signal.py --input &lt;samples.jsonl> --output-dir &lt;dir> --dataset &lt;name> --experiment &lt;name>\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse, json, os, sys\nimport numpy as np\nfrom collections import Counter\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\nfrom consistency import structural_consistency_soft_jaccard, fleiss_kappa_surface\nfrom evaluation import per_instance_f1\n\ndef parse_args():\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    p.add_argument(\"--input\", required=True, help=\"Path to samples.jsonl\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--output-dir\", required=True)\n    p.add_argument(\"--dataset\", default=\"unknown\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--experiment\", default=\"unknown\")\n    p.add_argument(\"--n-samples\", type=int, default=8)\n    p.add_argument(\"--gold-filter\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Skip instances with empty gold\")\n    return p.parse_args()\n\ndef main():\n    args = parse_args()\n    sys.stdout = open(sys.stdout.fileno(), mode='w', buffering=1)\n\n    print(f\"Loading {args.input}...\")\n    instances = []\n    with open(args.input) as f:\n        for line in f:\n            if line.strip():\n                instances.append(json.loads(line))\n\n    n_total_raw = len(instances)\n    if args.gold_filter:\n        instances = [inst for inst in instances if len(inst[\"gold\"].get(\"entities\", [])) > 0]\n    n_total = len(instances)\n    print(f\"Instances: {n_total} (raw={n_total_raw}, gold_filter={args.gold_filter})\")\n\n    N = args.n_samples\n\n    # Phase 1: Greedy and Oracle F1\n    print(\"Phase 1: Computing greedy/oracle F1...\")\n    greedy_f1s = np.zeros(n_total)\n    oracle_f1s = np.zeros(n_total)\n    all_sample_f1s = np.zeros((n_total, N))\n\n    for i, inst in enumerate(instances):\n        greedy_f1s[i] = per_instance_f1(inst[\"greedy\"], inst[\"gold\"], subtask=\"ner\")\n        for j, s in enumerate(inst[\"samples\"][:N]):\n            all_sample_f1s[i, j] = per_instance_f1(s, inst[\"gold\"], subtask=\"ner\")\n        oracle_f1s[i] = all_sample_f1s[i].max()\n        if (i+1) % 5000 == 0:\n            print(f\"  {i+1}/{n_total}\")\n\n    greedy_macro_f1 = float(greedy_f1s.mean())\n    oracle_macro_f1 = float(oracle_f1s.mean())\n    headroom = oracle_macro_f1 - greedy_macro_f1\n\n    print(f\"Greedy macro F1: {greedy_macro_f1:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Oracle macro F1: {oracle_macro_f1:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Headroom: {headroom:.4f}\")\n\n    # Phase 2: 5 signals\n    print(\"\\nPhase 2: Computing 5 signals...\")\n    lp_values = np.zeros(n_total)\n    sj_values = np.zeros(n_total)\n    fk_values = np.zeros(n_total)\n    em_values = np.zeros(n_total)\n    vc_values = np.zeros(n_total)\n\n    for i, inst in enumerate(instances):\n        samples = inst[\"samples\"][:N]\n        n = len(samples)\n\n        lps = [s.get(\"mean_logprob\") for s in samples if s.get(\"mean_logprob\") is not None]\n        lp_values[i] = float(np.mean(lps)) if lps else float(\"nan\")\n\n        sj_values[i] = structural_consistency_soft_jaccard(samples, subtask=\"ner\")\n        fk_values[i] = fleiss_kappa_surface(samples, subtask=\"ner\")\n\n        sample_keys = []\n        for s in samples:\n            keys = frozenset((e.get(\"text\",\"\"), e.get(\"type\",\"\")) for e in s.get(\"entities\", []))\n            sample_keys.append(keys)\n        match_count = sum(1 for a in range(n) for b in range(a+1, n) if sample_keys[a] == sample_keys[b])\n        em_values[i] = match_count / max(n*(n-1)//2, 1)\n\n        counts = Counter(len(s.get(\"entities\", [])) for s in samples)\n        vc_values[i] = 1.0 - (len(counts) - 1) / max(n - 1, 1)\n\n        if (i+1) % 5000 == 0:\n            print(f\"  {i+1}/{n_total}\")\n\n    # Phase 3: Signal-based selection\n    print(\"\\nPhase 3: Signal-based sample selection...\")\n    signals = {\"LP\": lp_values, \"SJ\": sj_values, \"FK\": fk_values, \"EM\": em_values, \"VC\": vc_values}\n\n    selection_results = {}\n    for sig_name, sig_vals in signals.items():\n        sel_f1 = np.zeros(n_total)\n        for i, inst in enumerate(instances):\n            samples = inst[\"samples\"][:N]\n            if sig_name == \"LP\":\n                lps_i = [s.get(\"mean_logprob\", float(\"-inf\")) for s in samples]\n                best_idx = int(np.argmax(lps_i))\n            else:\n                best_idx = 0\n            sel_f1[i] = all_sample_f1s[i, best_idx]\n        selection_results[sig_name] = float(sel_f1.mean())\n\n    # Phase 4: Correlations\n    print(\"\\nPhase 4: Signal correlations...\")\n    from scipy.stats import spearmanr\n\n    cond_mask = (all_sample_f1s.max(axis=1) > 0)\n    corr_results = {}\n    for sig_name, sig_vals in signals.items():\n        valid = ~np.isnan(sig_vals)\n        if valid.sum() > 10:\n            rho, p = spearmanr(sig_vals[valid], greedy_f1s[valid])\n        else:\n            rho, p = 0.0, 1.0\n        combined = valid & cond_mask\n        if combined.sum() > 10:\n            cond_rho, cond_p = spearmanr(sig_vals[combined], greedy_f1s[combined])\n        else:\n            cond_rho, cond_p = 0.0, 1.0\n        corr_results[sig_name] = {\n            \"global_rho\": round(float(rho), 4),\n            \"global_p\": float(p),\n            \"conditional_rho\": round(float(cond_rho), 4),\n            \"conditional_p\": float(cond_p),\n        }\n\n    # Phase 5: DGS\n    print(\"\\nPhase 5: DGS (Degeneracy-Gated Selection)...\")\n    dgs_f1 = np.zeros(n_total)\n    n_degenerate = 0\n    for i, inst in enumerate(instances):\n        samples = inst[\"samples\"][:N]\n        sample_f1_set = set(round(f, 10) for f in all_sample_f1s[i])\n        is_degenerate = len(sample_f1_set) == 1\n\n        if is_degenerate:\n            n_degenerate += 1\n            dgs_f1[i] = greedy_f1s[i]\n        else:\n            lps_i = [s.get(\"mean_logprob\", float(\"-inf\")) for s in samples]\n            best_idx = int(np.argmax(lps_i))\n            dgs_f1[i] = all_sample_f1s[i, best_idx]\n\n    dgs_macro_f1 = float(dgs_f1.mean())\n    dgs_delta = dgs_macro_f1 - greedy_macro_f1\n    degen_rate = n_degenerate / n_total\n\n    # Summary\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*70)\n    print(f\"{args.experiment.upper()} 5-SIGNAL ANALYSIS SUMMARY\")\n    print(\"=\"*70)\n    print(f\"Dataset: {args.dataset}\")\n    print(f\"Instances: {n_total}\")\n    print(f\"N (samples per instance): {N}\")\n    print(f\"Greedy macro F1: {greedy_macro_f1:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Oracle macro F1: {oracle_macro_f1:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Headroom: {headroom:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"F1>0 instances: {int(cond_mask.sum())}/{n_total}\")\n    print()\n    print(f\"{'Signal':<6} {'Global ρ':>10} {'Cond ρ':>10} {'Sel F1':>10} {'Δ vs greedy':>12}\")\n    print(\"-\"*52)\n    for sig_name in [\"LP\", \"SJ\", \"FK\", \"EM\", \"VC\"]:\n        g_rho = corr_results[sig_name][\"global_rho\"]\n        c_rho = corr_results[sig_name][\"conditional_rho\"]\n        s_f1 = selection_results[sig_name]\n        delta = s_f1 - greedy_macro_f1\n        print(f\"{sig_name:<6} {g_rho:>10.4f} {c_rho:>10.4f} {s_f1:>10.4f} {delta:>+12.4f}\")\n    print()\n    print(f\"DGS macro F1: {dgs_macro_f1:.4f} (Δ = {dgs_delta:+.4f})\")\n    print(f\"Degeneracy rate: {degen_rate:.4f} ({n_degenerate}/{n_total})\")\n\n    # Save\n    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)\n    results = {\n        \"dataset\": args.dataset,\n        \"experiment\": args.experiment,\n        \"n_total\": n_total,\n        \"n_total_raw\": n_total_raw,\n        \"gold_filter\": args.gold_filter,\n        \"n_samples_per_instance\": N,\n        \"greedy_macro_f1\": round(greedy_macro_f1, 4),\n        \"oracle_macro_f1\": round(oracle_macro_f1, 4),\n        \"headroom\": round(headroom, 4),\n        \"n_f1_positive\": int(cond_mask.sum()),\n        \"signals\": {},\n        \"dgs\": {\n            \"macro_f1\": round(dgs_macro_f1, 4),\n            \"delta\": round(dgs_delta, 4),\n            \"degeneracy_rate\": round(degen_rate, 4),\n            \"n_degenerate\": n_degenerate,\n        },\n    }\n    for sig_name in [\"LP\", \"SJ\", \"FK\", \"EM\", \"VC\"]:\n        results[\"signals\"][sig_name] = {\n            **corr_results[sig_name],\n            \"selection_f1\": round(selection_results[sig_name], 4),\n            \"selection_delta\": round(selection_results[sig_name] - greedy_macro_f1, 4),\n        }\n\n    out_path = os.path.join(args.output_dir, \"5signal_dgs_analysis.json\")\n    with open(out_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"\\nSaved to {out_path}\")\n    print(\"DONE\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nPYEOF\necho \"Created analyze_generic_5signal.py\"",
  "warn": true
}
